<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every nonexpansive selfmap of a nonempty closed bounded convex subset of a real reflexive Banach space has a fixed point. This resolves the reflexive-space fixed point problem for the given norm.

- **claim-reflexive-fixed-point** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a real reflexive Banach space, and let $C\subseteq X$ be nonempty, norm closed, bounded, and convex. If $F:C\to C$ satisfies $$\norm{F(a)-F(b)}\le \norm{a-b}\qquad(a,b\in C),$$ then $F$ has a fixed point.
  - Explanation: The asserted fixed point property concerns the original norm on a real reflexive Banach space and nonexpansive selfmaps of the specified convex domains.
- **claim-commuting-common-retract** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-commuting)): Let $X$ be a real reflexive Banach space, and let $C\subseteq X$ be nonempty, norm closed, bounded, and convex. For any pairwise commuting family $\mathcal S$ of nonexpansive selfmaps of $C$, the common fixed set $$\operatorname{Fix}(\mathcal S) =\{x\in C:T(x)=x\text{ for every }T\in\mathcal S\}$$ is nonempty and is a nonexpansive retract of $C$. That is, there is a nonexpansive map $R:C\to\operatorname{Fix}(\mathcal S)$ that is the identity on $\operatorname{Fix}(\mathcal S)$.
  - Explanation: A pairwise commuting family has a nonempty common fixed set admitting a nonexpansive retraction. The proof invokes the hereditary and conditional fixed point properties obtained from the main theorem.
- **claim-weak-separation-and-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-facts)): Let $X$ be a real normed space.* 1. *Every $v\in X$ has a norming functional: there is $f\in X^*$ with $\norm{f}\leq 1$ and $f(v)=\norm{v}$. Every norm-closed convex set is weakly closed, and the norm is weakly lower semicontinuous.* 2. *On a finite Cartesian power of $X$, the weak topology is the product weak topology. The norm and weak closures of a convex set coincide.* 3. *If $X$ is reflexive and complete, every norm-closed bounded convex subset of $X$ is weakly compact. Arbitrary products of compact Hausdorff spaces are compact.
  - Explanation: The foundational lemma supplies norming functionals, weak closedness and lower semicontinuity, finite-product topology and closure facts, reflexive weak compactness, and product compactness.
- **claim-minimal-invariant-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-reduction)): If Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> fails, there are a nonempty separable, weakly compact convex set $K$ and a nonexpansive map $T:K\to K$ without a fixed point such that $K$ has no proper nonempty norm-closed convex $T$-invariant subset. We may take $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}K=1$ by scalar normalization. Moreover, the weak topology on $K$ is metrizable. For $$Y=\overline{\operatorname{span}}(K-K), \qquad B_Y=\{y\in Y:\norm{y}\leq1\},$$ the ball $B_Y$ is weakly compact, and $Y$ is separable.
  - Explanation: Assuming a counterexample, the argument reduces to a separable minimal invariant convex set with normalized diameter and metrizable weak topology. The associated closed difference span has a weakly compact unit ball.
- **claim-approximate-points-diametral** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-diametral)): Every approximate fixed point sequence $(u_j)$ satisfies $$\lim_{j\to\infty}\norm{u_j-z}=1 \qquad(z\in K).$$
  - Explanation: In the hypothetical minimal invariant set, every approximate fixed point sequence approaches the normalized diameter in distance from each prescribed point.
- **claim-single-functional-compact-detector** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-detector)): For every nonempty norm-compact set $E\subseteq K$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\delta>0$ such that each $y\in K$ with $\norm{Ty-y}\leq\delta$ admits a single $f\in X^*$, $\norm{f}\leq1$, satisfying $$f(y-z)\geq1-\varepsilon\qquad(z\in E).$$
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small fixed point defect permits a single bounded functional to detect differences from an entire prescribed norm-compact set. The required defect threshold depends on that set and the requested error.
- **claim-resolvent-existence-and-control** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-resolvents)): For every $n\geq 1$ and $a\in K$, there is a unique point $R_n(a)\in K$ satisfying $$\label{eq:resolvent-definition} R_n(a)=\frac{a}{n}+\left(1-\frac1n\right)T R_n(a).$$ The map $R_n:K\to K$ is nonexpansive, and $$\label{eq:resolvent-estimates} \norm{T R_n(a)-R_n(a)}\leq\frac1n, \qquad \norm{R_n(a)-a}\leq(n-1)\norm{Ta-a} \quad(a\in K).$$
  - Explanation: The resolvent equation has a unique solution for each admissible argument and index. Its solution map is nonexpansive, with explicit defect and displacement bounds.
- **claim-finite-simultaneous-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-tuples)): Let $\mathcal B\subseteq K^K$ be a nonempty convex family of nonexpansive maps. Every map $h\in\overline{\mathcal B}^{\,\mathrm{pw}}$ is nonexpansive. For any integer $q\geq1$, points $a_1,\ldots,a_q\in K$, and $\delta>0$, there is a single $g\in\mathcal B$ such that $$\max_{1\leq i\leq q}\norm{g(a_i)-h(a_i)}<\delta.$$
  - Explanation: Pointwise weak closure preserves nonexpansiveness. Convexity then upgrades finite simultaneous weak approximation to norm approximation at the prescribed arguments.
- **claim-compact-semigroup-idempotent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-idempotent)): Let $S$ be a nonempty compact Hausdorff semigroup. Suppose that, for each $p\in S$, the right translation $h\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}\OriginalMapsto\EndAccSupp{}hp$ is continuous. Then $S$ contains an idempotent: an element $p$ with $p^2=p$.
  - Explanation: A nonempty compact Hausdorff semigroup with continuous right translations contains an idempotent. The proof selects a minimal compact subsemigroup.
- **claim-idempotent-anchor-return** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-anchor)): The set $A$ is a nonempty compact semigroup of nonexpansive selfmaps of $K$ and contains an idempotent $P$. There is a point $x\in K$ with $P(x)=x$ such that the nonempty weakly compact set $$\label{eq:anchor-tail-set} D_x=\bigcap_{m\geq1} \overline{\{h(x):h\in\mathcal H_m\}}^{\,\mathrm{weak}}$$ satisfies $$\label{eq:anchor-convex-hull} x\in\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}D_x}^{\,\norm{\cdot}}.$$
  - Explanation: The asymptotic map family forms a compact semigroup. An idempotent supplies an anchor lying in the norm-closed convex hull of its tail weak cluster points.
- **claim-adaptive-finite-anchor-selection** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-adaptive-selection)): Let $\tau>0$. There is a successive selection procedure with the following property. Before the $i$th map is chosen, prescribe any finite integer $m_i\geq1$, allowing this prescription to depend on the entire previous history and on any auxiliary data already fixed at that stage. The procedure chooses $G_i\in\mathcal H_{m_i}$ and terminates after finitely many steps, say $M$, with nonnegative coefficients $\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_M$ such that $$\label{eq:adaptive-return} \sum_{i=1}^M\beta_i=1, \qquad \norm*{\sum_{i=1}^M\beta_iG_i(x)-x}<\tau.$$ The coefficients are assigned on termination. Removing zero coefficients gives a nonempty list with positive coefficients, in the original order of selection.
  - Explanation: Successively prescribed thresholds may depend on all earlier choices. A finite selected list nevertheless admits convex coefficients returning its anchor images within the requested tolerance; the coefficients are chosen only at termination.
- **claim-infinite-ordered-tree-data** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-tree-data)): There is an infinite ordered tree with the maps, points, and positive weights described above. Each nonroot node $v$ also has a budget $e_v>0$, a functional $f_v\in X^*$ with $\norm{f_v}\le1$, and the finite earlier-candidate list and predictions just defined. These data have the following properties:* 1. *The budgets satisfy $$\sum_{v\ne r}e_v\le\frac1{128}. \label{eq:tree-budget}$$ Define $$\tau_0=\frac12,\qquad \tau_k=2^{-k-1} \min\{e_u:u\in L_i,\ 1\le i\le k\}\quad(k\ge1). \label{eq:tree-tolerance}$$ Every node $u\in L_k$ satisfies $$\norm{\sum_{\widehat v=u}\alpha_v y_v-x}\le\tau_k. \label{eq:child-average}$$* 2. *For every nonroot node $v$, $$\sum_{p\prec v}\norm{G_p(y_v)-y_v}\le e_v, \qquad f_v(y_v-z)\ge1-e_v\quad(z\in E_v). \label{eq:tree-conditions}$$ In particular, the second inequality holds at every $z_i$ with $i\le k$ when $v\in L_k$.* *The budgets, functionals, and prediction sets assigned to retained nodes remain fixed when the remaining weights at their level and all later levels are chosen.
  - Explanation: The inductive tree carries positive averaging weights, summable budgets, ancestor displacement control, and compact-set detecting functionals. Retained prediction data remain fixed as later choices are made.
- **claim-finite-branch-witness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-witness)): For every $N\geq 1$, there are a leaf $s\in L_N$ and a vector $d\in B_Y$ such that $$f_v(d)\geq \frac12 \qquad (r\ne v\preceq s).$$
  - Explanation: Every finite truncation of the constructed tree has a leaf and a unit-ball vector detected uniformly by all nonroot functionals on that leaf's ancestral path. The vector comes from a normalized actual root-output difference.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **fixed point property for nonexpansive maps** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Banach space has the *fixed point property for nonexpansive maps* if every nonexpansive selfmap of each nonempty closed bounded convex subset has a fixed point.
- **weak topology** ([source](#sec:prelim)): The weak topology of a normed space is the topology generated by its continuous linear functionals.
- **reflexivity** ([source](#sec:prelim)): Reflexivity means that the canonical map $J_X:X\to X^{**}$, defined by $(J_Xx)(f)=f(x)$, is onto.
- **norm-closed convex hull** ([source](#sec:prelim)): We write $\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}} A$ for the norm-closed convex hull of $A$.
- **proper filter** ([source](#sec:prelim)): A proper filter is a family of subsets closed under finite intersections and supersets, containing the whole set but not the empty set.
- **ultrafilter** ([source](#sec:prelim)): An *ultrafilter* is maximal under inclusion among proper filters.
- **ultrafilter limit** ([source](#sec:prelim)): For a sequence $(a_j)$, the notation $a=\lim_{j\to\mathcal U}a_j$ means that $\{j:a_j\in V\}\in\mathcal U$ for every neighborhood $V$ of $a$.
- **approximate fixed point sequence** ([source](#sec:prelim)): A sequence $(u_j)$ in $K$ is an *approximate fixed point sequence* if $\norm{Tu_j-u_j}\to0$.
- **resolvent** ([source](#stmt-lem-resolvents)): For every $n\geq 1$ and $a\in K$, there is a unique point $R_n(a)\in K$ satisfying $$\label{eq:resolvent-definition} R_n(a)=\frac{a}{n}+\left(1-\frac1n\right)T R_n(a).$$
- **generated family of maps** ([source](#eq:map-displacement)): Let $\mathcal G$ be the family generated by the identity map and the maps $R_n$ using finitely many compositions and pointwise finite convex combinations.
- **tail family** ([source](#eq:tail-defect)): For $m\geq1$, define the tail family $$\label{eq:tail-family} \mathcal H_m =\{R_n\circ G:n\geq m,\ G\in\mathcal G\}.$$
- **compact asymptotic map family** ([source](#eq:map-closures)): $$\label{eq:map-closures} A_m=\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\mathcal H_m}^{\,\mathrm{pw}}, \qquad A=\bigcap_{m\geq1}A_m.$$
- **path weights** ([source](#eq:tree-sensitivity)): Define the path weights by $$W_r=1,\qquad W_v=W_{\widehat v}\alpha_v\quad(v\ne r).$$
- **feasible node** ([source](#sec:switching)): Call a node $v$ *feasible* if some $d\in B_Y$ satisfies $$f_u(d)\geq\frac12\qquad(r\ne u\preceq v).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-normalized-diameter | \mathop{\mathrm{diam}}K | = | $1$ | Scalar normalization of the original norm. |
| q-diametral-limit | \lim_{j\to\infty}\norm{u_j-z} | = | $1$ | Every prescribed point of the set; no convergence modulus is stated. |
| q-compact-detector-lower-bound | f(y-z) | ≥ | $1-\varepsilon$ | The functional may depend on the approximate fixed point; the defect threshold depends on the prescribed compact set and error. |
| q-resolvent-defect | \norm{T R_n(a)-R_n(a)} | ≤ | $\frac1n$ | Every admissible resolvent index and argument. |
| q-resolvent-displacement | \norm{R_n(a)-a} | ≤ | $(n-1)\norm{Ta-a}$ | Every admissible index and argument; displacement is controlled by the argument's original defect. |
| q-generated-map-displacement | \norm{G(a)-a} | ≤ | $c_G\norm{Ta-a}$ | The finite constant depends on the generated map and is uniform over its arguments. |
| q-tail-defect | \norm{T h(a)-h(a)} | ≤ | $\frac1m$ | Uniform in the tail map and its argument at a prescribed tail threshold. |
| q-tree-total-budget | \sum_{v\ne r}e_v | ≤ | $\frac1{128}$ | The sum ranges over all nonroot retained nodes. |
| q-finite-witness-threshold | f_v(d) | ≥ | $\frac12$ | Every nonroot ancestor of the selected leaf; the selected leaf and vector may depend on the truncation. |
| q-block-detection-deficit | f_v(U_v-V_v) | ≥ | $W_v-5e_v$ | The common terminal point is selected after all data in the finite truncation are fixed. |
| q-total-weighted-deficit | \sum_{s\in L_N}W_s \sum_{r\ne v\preceq s}\bigl(1-f_v(d_s)\bigr) | ≤ | $\frac{5}{128}$ | All leaves and nonroot ancestral constraints in the fixed finite truncation. |
| q-failing-leaf-weight | total weight of all such leaves | ≤ | $5/64$ | Total path-weight mass of failing leaves in a fixed finite truncation. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-foundations** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-facts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-witness-3): Establish extension and separation, identify finite-product weak topology, and derive compactness through ultrafilters and reflexivity. -> The separation, closure, and compactness facts needed by the argument.
- **step-minimal-invariant-set** [claim](#stmt-lem-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reduction): Generate a separable invariant convex domain, apply minimality by reversed inclusion, normalize its diameter, and metrize its weak topology using separating functionals. -> A normalized separable minimal invariant set and a weakly compact difference-space unit ball.
- **step-diametral-sequences** [claim](#stmt-lem-diametral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diametral): Use minimality to make convex invariant radius functions constant; weak limits determine their value, and the same reasoning for subsequences establishes the limit. -> Asymptotically diametral distances from every prescribed point.
- **step-compact-detector** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-detector): Take a finite net in the prescribed norm-compact set, average its points, and use a norming functional at an approximate fixed point far from that average. -> An existential defect threshold ensuring a common detecting functional.
- **step-resolvents** [claim](#stmt-lem-resolvents) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-resolvents): Iterate the affine contraction defining each resolvent; compare its fixed point equations to obtain nonexpansiveness and displacement bounds. -> Resolvents with uniform defect and argument displacement control.
- **step-finite-tuples** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-tuples) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-tuples): Use weak closedness of norm balls for nonexpansiveness and equality of weak and norm closures of a convex evaluation set for simultaneous approximation. -> Finite simultaneous norm approximation of maps in the pointwise weak closure.
- **step-semigroup-idempotent** [claim](#stmt-lem-idempotent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-idempotent): Select a minimal compact subsemigroup and use compact right translates and stabilizers to force idempotence. -> An idempotent in the compact semigroup.
- **step-anchor** [claim](#stmt-prop-anchor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-anchor): Build compact closures of convexified tails, prove composition closure by finite simultaneous approximation, obtain an idempotent, and separate the anchor from its tail cluster hull if necessary. -> An anchor fixed by an idempotent and contained in its tail cluster convex hull.
- **step-adaptive-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-adaptive-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-adaptive-selection): Cycle through finitely many weak cluster targets while accepting history-dependent thresholds; failure to terminate would contradict the anchor's convex-hull property. -> A finite selected list with convex coefficients returning close to the anchor.
- **step-prediction-sets** [claim](#stmt-prop-tree-data) / [proof](#eq:prediction-lipschitz): Parameterize ideal cutoff outputs by earlier candidate coefficients, take weak limits of the already evaluated outputs, and use a uniform Lipschitz bound to obtain norm-compact prediction images. -> Norm-compact detector sets determined before the current candidate map and later sibling weights.
- **step-ordered-tree** [claim](#stmt-prop-tree-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tree-data): Assign summable candidate budgets, impose displacement and detector thresholds before selection, and assign sibling weights only after adaptive selection terminates. -> The infinite ordered tree with positive weights and persistent functional and prediction data.
- **step-finite-witness** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-witness): Choose a common terminal point for the finite truncation, switch leaves from right to left, bound actual block differences, and sum nonnegative weighted deficits to select a good leaf. -> A leaf and a normalized root-difference vector detected along its whole ancestral path.
- **step-infinite-branch-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-witness-2): Extract an infinite feasible branch, intersect its nested weakly closed unit-ball constraints, and contradict the resulting uniform detection using pointwise vanishing on a dense difference span. -> The hypothetical counterexample is excluded, yielding the asserted fixed point theorem.
- **step-commuting-retraction** [claim](#stmt-cor-commuting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-commuting): Apply the main theorem to invariant convex subdomains to obtain hereditary and conditional fixed point properties; then invoke the cited commuting-family retraction theorem. -> A nonempty common fixed set with a nonexpansive retraction; the empty family is handled by the identity.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fixed-point-existence**: The stated nonexpansive selfmap has a fixed point in its domain, with nonexpansiveness measured in the given norm.
- **invariant-common-fixed-retract**: A pairwise commuting family of nonexpansive selfmaps has a nonempty common fixed set that is a nonexpansive retract.
- **invariant-resolvent-control**: The resolvent equation has a unique solution, and the resulting nonexpansive map satisfies the stated defect and displacement bounds.
- **invariant-compact-detector**: A sufficiently accurate approximate fixed point admits a single bounded functional detecting its differences from every point of a prescribed norm-compact set.
- **invariant-adaptive-finite-return**: Successively prescribed thresholds may depend on the previous history; the selection eventually admits a finite convex combination returning within the requested tolerance of the anchor.
- **invariant-finite-path-witness**: Every finite truncation of the constructed tree admits a leaf and a unit-ball vector detected by every nonroot functional on its path.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-weak-compactness-alone**: Replace reflexivity of the ambient space by weak compactness of the domain alone. -> The paper explicitly identifies a weakly compact convex domain in a nonreflexive space with a fixed-point-free nonexpansive selfmap.
- **anti-noncommuting-family**: Conclude that an arbitrary family of individually nonexpansive maps has a common fixed point. -> Individual fixed-point existence does not establish a common fixed set under the stated corollary without its family hypothesis.
- **anti-resolvent-as-finite-exact-solver**: Treat the resolvent construction as a terminating exact solver for the original fixed point. -> The resolvent solves an auxiliary equation and has a controlled fixed-point defect; the proof obtains its solution through convergence of contraction iterates.
- **anti-effective-adaptive-budget**: Use finite adaptive selection as an executable procedure with a known stopping budget. -> The proof starts with points in a compact tail intersection and uses weak-neighborhood choices; its termination argument contradicts hypothetical nontermination rather than supplying a quantitative stopping bound.
- **anti-nonlinear-weak-limit-interchange**: Move a weak limit through the nonlinear maps used to evaluate the tree. -> The prediction construction takes weak limits of already evaluated root outputs; nonexpansiveness alone is not a supplied weak-continuity certificate.
- **anti-finite-witness-as-global-certificate**: Regard a witness on a finite tree truncation as certification of the entire infinite-branch contradiction. -> The final argument additionally extracts an infinite feasible branch and uses weak compactness to obtain a common vector before applying pointwise convergence of the functionals.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves an abstract existence theorem through minimal invariant sets, compact semigroups, weak ultrafilter limits, and an infinite tree. The compact-semigroup proof states: “Zorn’s lemma therefore gives a minimal nonempty compact subsemigroup $S'\subseteq S$.” The resolvent construction uses a limit of contraction iterates, and adaptive finite selection relies on previously chosen compact tail-cluster points. The finite witness proposition has finite depth but assumes maps and functionals obtained from this nonconstructive infinite construction; it does not specify a finite effective input representation. Checking a separately supplied finite tree would certify only that instance, rather than the existence and compactness steps required by the paper.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fixed point assertion supplies existence under geometric hypotheses, but gives no finite representation of arbitrary inputs or executable contract for returning a fixed point. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ordered tree organizes a contradiction argument using abstract maps and separating functionals; it does not specify a game state, physical evolution, or simulation update model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Adaptive selection terminates in the mathematical argument, but its choices depend on weak closure membership and an existential anchor rather than supplied computational access to those objects. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The resolvent proof explicitly iterates a contraction formed from the anchor argument and the nonexpansive map. This gives an approximation construction when evaluations, convex combinations, and norm errors are available, although the paper supplies no general finite representation or exact termination contract. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite simultaneous approximation concerns tuples of values of abstract maps and equality of convex weak and norm closures. It does not provide a storage representation, supported operations, or access costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite witness statement quantifies over a finite tree level but its vectors belong to an abstract Banach space. No coordinate representation, arithmetic workload, or parallel execution construction is supplied. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact semigroup lemma asserts existence of an idempotent under topological continuity assumptions. It supplies neither a cryptographic construction nor a computational hardness or adversary model. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact detector produces a separating functional within the hypothetical minimal invariant set argument. It specifies no learning objective, training data, or effective procedure for fitting that functional. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The anchor lies in a norm-closed convex hull of weak cluster points, but the proposition supplies no optimization objective, feasible input encoding, or effective method for choosing the idempotent and cluster points. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The commuting-family corollary gives a common fixed set and a nonexpansive retraction. It supplies no communication protocol, update schedule, or convergence argument for distributed execution. |
| other (Functional analysis and metric fixed point theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper constructs resolvent selfmaps and proves their nonexpansiveness and displacement estimates. These are mathematical constructions for studying approximate fixed points in the stated setting, with effective numerical realization requiring additional input assumptions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ambient-reflexivity** (hypothesis): The main assertion requires a real reflexive Banach space. Weak compactness of a convex domain in an arbitrary Banach space is explicitly insufficient. Blocked use: ['Apply the main assertion solely because the domain is weakly compact.', 'Extend the assertion to nonreflexive ambient spaces without another theorem.']
- **limit-domain-conditions** (hypothesis): The domain must be nonempty, norm closed, bounded, and convex. The assertion does not authorize removing any of these conditions. Blocked use: ['Apply the assertion to an empty, nonclosed, unbounded, or nonconvex domain without a separate justification.']
- **limit-nonexpansive-selfmap** (hypothesis): The map must preserve the domain and satisfy the stated nonexpansive inequality throughout that domain. Blocked use: ['Use a map that sends some domain points outside the domain.', 'Replace the global nonexpansive condition by checks on selected samples.']
- **limit-specified-norm** (formal_scope): Nonexpansiveness is measured in the given norm. An equivalent renorming can change which maps satisfy the required inequality. Blocked use: ['Transfer nonexpansiveness from an equivalent norm without checking it in the norm used by the assertion.']
- **limit-commuting-family** (hypothesis): The common fixed set and retraction conclusion requires pairwise commuting nonexpansive selfmaps of the same admissible domain. Blocked use: ['Infer a common fixed point from separate fixed point existence for a noncommuting family.', 'Apply the retraction conclusion to maps with different invariant domains.']
- **limit-counterfactual-minimal-setting** (formal_scope): The minimal invariant set and subsequent diametral assertions belong to the assumed-counterexample argument. They are not unconditional properties of arbitrary invariant convex sets. Blocked use: ['Apply the diametral conclusion to an arbitrary approximate fixed point sequence outside the minimal invariant setting.', 'Treat the fixed-point-free minimal set as an object supplied independently of the contradiction hypothesis.']
- **limit-diameter-normalization** (formal_scope): The displayed resolvent defect bounds use the scalar-normalized norm on the minimal invariant set. Their numerical form must not be transferred unchanged to an unnormalized domain. Blocked use: ['Use the stated defect estimate in original distance units without accounting for the normalization.']
- **limit-norm-compact-detector-set** (hypothesis): The common detecting functional is asserted for a nonempty norm-compact comparison set and a prescribed positive error. The defect threshold depends on these inputs and is obtained existentially. Blocked use: ['Replace norm compactness by weak compactness when invoking the compact detector.', 'Assume a uniform computable defect threshold for arbitrary comparison sets.']
- **limit-resolvent-versus-original-fixed-point** (approximation): A resolvent solves an auxiliary equation and has a controlled defect for the original map. Its contraction iteration converges to the auxiliary solution; this does not establish exact finite termination or convergence of ordinary iterates of the original map. Blocked use: ['Return a resolvent value as an exact fixed point of the original map solely from the defect bound.', 'Claim that ordinary iteration of the original nonexpansive map converges because the auxiliary contraction iterates converge.']
- **limit-finite-simultaneous-approximation** (hypothesis): Simultaneous norm approximation requires a nonempty convex family of nonexpansive maps and a finite tuple of arguments. The conclusion supplies an approximating map existentially. Blocked use: ['Drop convexity when upgrading pointwise weak approximation to simultaneous norm approximation.', 'Replace the finite tuple by all arguments and claim uniform norm approximation.']
- **limit-semigroup-idempotent-selection** (nonconstructive): The idempotent argument requires a nonempty compact Hausdorff semigroup with continuous right translations. Its proof selects a minimal compact subsemigroup by maximality rather than providing an effective search. Blocked use: ['Invoke idempotent existence after checking only associativity.', 'Treat the compact semigroup argument as a finite algorithm for computing the anchor.']
- **limit-adaptive-selection-effectivity** (nonconstructive): Adaptive selection allows finite thresholds prescribed from previous history and assigns convex coefficients only upon termination. Its finite termination depends on the previously established anchor and weak cluster targets; an effective stopping bound is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Advertise a computable running-time guarantee for the adaptive selection.', 'Fix the convex coefficients before selection while claiming the stated history-dependent guarantee.', 'Use the selection without access to admissible tail maps and the anchor construction.']
- **limit-nonlinear-prediction-order** (formal_scope): Predictions take weak limits after evaluating root outputs. The construction supplies no permission to pass weak limits through nonlinear maps. Blocked use: ['Replace a weak limit of evaluated tree outputs by evaluation at weak limits of their inputs without proving the required continuity.']
- **limit-persistent-tree-data** (hypothesis): The tree argument requires positive averaging weights, summable budgets, the prescribed tolerance schedule, ancestor displacement control, and compact-set detection. Retained functionals and prediction sets must remain fixed as later choices are made. Blocked use: ['Change retained functionals after observing later sibling weights.', 'Discard earlier candidate information needed by subsequent predictions.', 'Remove budget summability or the tolerance schedule while retaining the switching conclusion.']
- **limit-finite-witness-inputs** (nonconstructive): Finite depth does not make the witness proposition an effective finite construction. It assumes the previously constructed maps and functionals, and its proof chooses a common terminal point using ultrafilter conditions. Blocked use: ['Present the finite witness proposition as a solver accepting an arbitrary finite weighted tree.', 'Treat an instance check as verification of the existence of the required prediction sets or terminal point.']
- **limit-infinite-compactness-step** (nonconstructive): The final contradiction additionally requires an infinite feasible branch and weak compactness of the difference-space unit ball. Finite witnesses alone do not furnish the common vector in the infinite intersection. Blocked use: ['Declare the entire fixed point assertion certified from a finite collection of leaf-switching checks.', 'Replace weak compactness of the difference-space unit ball by boundedness alone.']
- **limit-unasserted-uniqueness-and-rates** (unknown): A general uniqueness conclusion, a convergence rate for original-map iterates, and an effective procedure for evaluating the common fixed set retraction are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Infer uniqueness of the fixed point from the main existence assertion.', 'Attach a convergence rate to ordinary nonexpansive iteration.', 'Treat existence of a nonexpansive retraction as an executable evaluation contract.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 328 (`lean/docs/328.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Fixed Points of Nonexpansive Maps in Reflexive Banach Spaces](../../preprints/Fixed-Points-of-Nonexpansive-Maps-in-Reflexive-Banach-Spaces-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The fixed-point question asks whether reflexivity suffices for nonexpansive maps on bounded convex sets. The formalized result gives an affirmative answer: every nonexpansive self-map of a nonempty closed bounded convex subset of a real reflexive Banach space has a fixed point in the original norm. Zero and nonseparable spaces are included, with no uniform convexity, normal structure, or weak-continuity assumption.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

namespace ReflexiveFixedPoints

universe u

variable (X : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace ℝ X]

/-- Surjectivity of the canonical map into the continuous bidual. -/
def CanonicallyReflexive : Prop :=
  Function.Surjective (NormedSpace.inclusionInDoubleDual ℝ X)

variable {X}

/-- Every nonexpansive selfmap of a nonempty closed bounded convex set has
an actual fixed point, with nonexpansiveness stated in the original norm. -/
theorem exists_fixedPoint_of_canonicallyReflexive
    {X : Type u} [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace ℝ X] [CompleteSpace X]
    (hX : CanonicallyReflexive X) (C : Set X)
    (hne : C.Nonempty) (hclosed : IsClosed C)
    (hbounded : Bornology.IsBounded C) (hconvex : Convex ℝ C)
    (F : C → C)
    (hF : ∀ a b : C, ‖(F a : X) - (F b : X)‖ ≤ ‖(a : X) - (b : X)‖) :
    ∃ a : C, F a = a := by
  sorry

end ReflexiveFixedPoints

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every nonexpansive selfmap of a nonempty closed bounded convex subset of a real reflexive Banach space has a fixed point. This resolves the reflexive-space fixed point problem for the given norm.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
